PROMPT 2026-10-15 15:22 UTC

keep

Claude's response · 1 action
  • Ran a command: Record R3; read R4

R3 is recorded as approved.

R4 of 26: A time exit that's already passed when the trade starts

Example: the strategy says "exit at 11:00", but a signal fills at 13:30. The 11:00 exit time has already gone.

What I did: for that trade, the 11:00 time exit simply doesn't apply. The trade still exits by its stop-loss, target, other exits or the 15:15 square-off.

The alternative would be to exit immediately at entry, since "11:00 has passed". That creates pointless trades that close the moment they open, each paying charges for nothing.

Recommendation: keep. It's the same logic as R23: a rule that's already passed isn't used for that trade. If you want, the cleaner way to stop late entries altogether is an entry window (for example, only enter before 11:00), which you can already set.

Keep?

87
PROMPT 2026-10-15 15:24 UTC

keep

Claude's response · 1 action
  • Ran a command: Record R4; read R5

R4 is recorded as approved.

R5 of 26: When "move stop to cost" takes effect

With 1-minute data, a single minute might show the premium both rising by enough to trigger "move the stop to cost" and falling back to your entry price. The data can't tell us which happened first.

What I did: the stop moves to cost from the next minute onwards. If the original stop-loss is hit in that same trigger minute, it counts as a normal stop-loss.

The alternative would be to assume the gain came first and the fall second, which would let the trade exit at cost in the same minute. That assumption flatters the results whenever the order is unknown.

Recommendation: keep. It's the conservative choice, the same principle the spec already uses: "if stop and target are hit in one minute, assume the stop came first".

Keep?

88
PROMPT 2026-10-15 15:26 UTC

keep

Claude's response · 1 action
  • Ran a command: Record R5; read R6

R5 is recorded as approved.

R6 of 26: Charges when a sold option expires in the money

When a trade is held to expiry and settles at intrinsic value instead of being closed, the spec lists the charges for a bought option that expires in the money, and for any option that expires worthless. It doesn't cover a sold option that expires in the money (assignment).

What I did: charge brokerage plus GST on that brokerage, and no STT, exchange, SEBI or stamp charges. My understanding is that at expiry STT on exercise falls on the buyer who holds the in-the-money option, not on the seller, and the stamp duty and exchange fees for an exercise aren't charged to the writer. These figures come from my own understanding, though, not from a circular I checked today. The release checklist already includes re-checking the charges against current NSE and broker notes.

Recommendation: keep, and verify it during that release check. This only affects short strategies held to expiry (positional), and the amounts are small next to the P&L. If a later check finds a charge that applies, it's a one-line change in the costs table.

Keep?

89
PROMPT 2026-10-15 15:30 UTC

In India (NSE), all stock options that expire In-The-Money (ITM) cannot simply be settled as options contracts. They trigger Physical Settlement (compulsory delivery of shares).

The exchange treats this as a regular equity delivery trade, meaning both the buyer and the writer (seller) face delivery charges.

Claude's response

You're right about stock options: in-the-money stock options on NSE are physically settled, and both sides pay delivery charges.

Nikasha only trades Nifty 50 index options, though, and index options are cash-settled. There are no shares to deliver for an index, so the difference is simply paid in cash at expiry. Physical settlement doesn't apply here. For a cash-settled in-the-money index option:

  • the buyer pays STT on exercise (0.125% of the intrinsic value), which Nikasha already charges;
  • the seller receives no delivery obligation, so the reading stands: brokerage plus GST, and no STT, exchange, SEBI or stamp charges.

If Nikasha later adds stock options, physical settlement would need its own rules: delivery charges for both sides and the margin needed. That's a real future item, but not something that applies today.

Recommendation still: keep R6, re-checked against current notes during the release checklist. Shall I record it as kept? I can also add a line to the future-scope section of product.md: "stock options would need physical-settlement charges (both sides)", so it isn't forgotten.

ok keep. no need to mention for stock options

Claude's response · 1 action

R6 is recorded as kept, with nothing added about stock options.

R7 of 26: The expiry calendar when a day's data is bad

Nikasha works out expiry dates from the trading days in the data. For example, if Tuesday is a holiday, expiry moves to Monday. Separately, a day whose data fails a quality check is excluded from the backtest.

The question: should an excluded day still count as a trading day for the expiry calendar?

What I did: yes. The market was open that day, and only our data for it is faulty. If it didn't count, a bad-data Tuesday would wrongly look like a holiday and shift that week's expiry to Monday.

Recommendation: keep. It keeps expiries matching the real exchange calendar. No day in the current data fails a check, so this changes nothing today; it only guards against future data problems.

Keep?

91
PROMPT 2026-10-15 15:32 UTC

keep

Claude's response · 1 action
  • Ran a command: Record R7; read R8

R7 is recorded as approved.

R8 of 26: Which moment's prices decide a "delta" or "premium" strike

There's an advanced option to pick the strike by delta (for example "the strike with delta about 0.3") or by premium (for example "the strike costing about ₹100"). Choosing that strike requires pricing several strikes at some moment.

What I did: price them at the moment the signal appears, that is, the index's closing price on the signal candle and India VIX at that minute. For fixed-time entries (such as 09:20), I use that minute's opening index and VIX. If VIX is missing at that moment, the trade is skipped and counted as "couldn't be priced" in the report, never guessed.

The alternative would be to use the next minute (the actual fill), but at signal time that minute hasn't happened yet, so choosing the strike from it would mean looking into the future.

Recommendation: keep. It's how a trader would actually choose: from what they see when the signal fires.

Keep?

92
PROMPT 2026-10-15 15:33 UTC

keep

Claude's response · 1 action
  • Ran a command: Record R8; read R9

R8 is recorded as approved.

R9 of 26: A tie between two strikes

When picking a strike by delta or premium, two strikes can match the target equally well. The spec says that in a tie, take the one nearer the at-the-money strike. But very rarely both are also exactly equally near at-the-money: one 50 points above and one 50 points below.

What I did: in that double tie, choose the lower strike. It's a fixed rule, so results are always repeatable.

Recommendation: keep. It almost never happens: both the target match and the distance from at-the-money must tie exactly. Any fixed choice works; it only needs to be consistent.

Keep?

Rules:

<section class="turn" id="c1-pN"><a class="num" href="#c1-pN">N</a><div class="body">
